# Multi-Level Implementation Task Table (WBS)

> **Companion design document**. This document accompanies
> [RFC-039 Compiler Architecture Refactoring](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md) and
> expands the RFC phase sequence into **independently committable and independently verifiable**
> three-level tasks.
>
> RFC-039 is the sole authority for phase ordering and acceptance gates; this document is
> responsible only for decomposing the work into an executable granularity and registering conflicts
> that remain undecided.

## Positioning and Scope

| Level       | Count                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ----------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| **Level 1** | 11 (P0–P10)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Phases, in one-to-one correspondence with RFC-039's phase sequence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **Level 2** | 72 (2026-10-13: 53 +2 = 3.13 / 3.14; 2026-10-14: +1 = 3.15; 2026-10-14: +1 = 3.16; 2026-10-15: +2 = 3.17 / 3.18; 2026-10-16: +1 = 0.4; 2026-10-18: +3 = 3.19 / S7 / S8; 2026-10-18: +2 = 3.21 / S9 (the original registration number for this group was 3.20; on 2026-10-21 it moved to 3.21, and 3.20 was reassigned to “pre-existing false positives in dead-code analysis”; see the closeout record in [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)); 2026-10-21: +2 = 0.5 / 3.20; 2026-10-21: +1 = S10 (the S9 number originally proposed for the 4.4 batch was already occupied by “abstracted closeout of the pre-release audit” and was therefore deferred); 2026-10-24: +1 = 3.22; 2026-10-25: +1 = 3.23; 2026-10-26: +1 = 9.12; 2026-10-27: +1 = S11)                                                                        | Task groups derived from the “implementation points” in the various documents + the 2026-10-07 audit addendum (3.4 in P3) + the 2026-10-10/11 decision registrations (3.5/3.6/3.7/3.8/3.9 in P3) + the 2026-10-12 registration (3.10 in P3) + the 2026-10-12 finding registration (3.11 in P3) + the 2026-10-12 additional finding registration (3.12 in P3) |
| **Level 3** | 193 (2026-10-13: 144 +2 = 3.13.1 / 3.14.1; 2026-10-14: +2 = 3.15.1 / 3.16.1; 2026-10-15: +2 = 3.17.1 / 3.18.1; 2026-10-16: +2 = 4.6.2 / 4.9.6 (`#450`, user decision A); 2026-10-16: +1 = 0.4.1; 2026-10-17: +1 = 4.9.7 (`#464`); 2026-10-18: +3 = 3.19.1 / S7.1 / S8.1; 2026-10-18: +12 = 4.9.8 / 3.14.2 / 4.10.4 / 4.10.5 / 3.21.1–3.21.4 (originally 3.20.1–3.20.4, renumbered on 2026-10-21) / S9.1 / S9.2 / S2.2 / 4.4.4; 2026-10-21: +7 = 3.6.2 / 3.6.3 / 3.6.4 / 0.5.1 / 0.5.2 / 3.20.1 / 3.20.2; 2026-10-21: +1 = S10.1; 2026-10-22: +5 = 3.6.5 / 3.6.6 / 4.10.6 / 4.10.7 / 4.10.8; 2026-10-24: +1 = 3.11.3; 2026-10-24: +1 = 0.5.3; 2026-10-24: +1 = 3.22.1; 2026-10-25: +2 = 3.23.1 / 3.18.2; +2 = 3.20.3 / 3.18.3; 2026-10-26: +1 = 3.20.4; 2026-10-26: +1 = 9.12.1; +1 = 9.12.2; 2026-10-27: +1 = S11.1) | Independently committable actions                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

**Unused numbers (not recycled)**: P4 has no `4.7`/`4.8`, and P8 has no `8.1`. None of the three had
a corresponding entry from the inception of the WBS—the entire history of this document has zero
matches for the three headings `**4.7` / `**4.8` / `**8.1`
(`git log -S'**4.7' -- docs/src/dev/architecture/09-execution-wbs.md` can be used for verification),
and there are no references anywhere else in the repository. `8.1` corresponds precisely to stage 1
of `05` (test revival and completion): that stage has already been split into P1 (`1.1`–`1.3`) and
`8.8`, so P8's numbering is aligned with its stage number (stage 0 → `8.0`, stage 2 → `8.2`, and so
forth).

**Covered by this document**: task decomposition, dependencies, parallelization groups, and binding
acceptance criteria.

**Not covered by this document**: the design rationale for tasks (see `02`–`08`) and the rationale
for phase ordering (see RFC-039).

**Registration convention (effective 2026-10-12)**: newly registered level-2/level-3 rows **must
contain two fields**—“**Owning phase**” (the P to which the task truly belongs, which may differ
from the section containing it) and “**Blocks the mainline**”; `登记待排期` is no longer the default
state, and an unmarked task is considered **not schedulable**. Numbers **must be unique throughout
the document** (tasks on separate tracks use their track number, such as `029g.x`, and must not
occupy `N.x`). **Checkoff/completion registration is based on “task id + evidence (gate values, run
id, issue number, file:line)” rather than a commit number**—`pull --rebase` rewrites local hashes,
so a hard-coded number will inevitably become invalid (2026-10-13 example: `94ad03ce4`→`631eb3e3c`).
Look up commit numbers with `git log`.

## Current State: Gaps Found During Decomposition

While consolidating the “implementation points” from eight documents into this table, **five hard
gaps in the documentation system itself** were found. All have now been resolved:

| #   | Gap                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Resolution                                                                                                                                                      |
| --- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| G1  | **P5 has no implementation steps whatsoever**. RFC defines P5 (checker decomposition) and its acceptance criterion (C1 zero-diff), but none of the seven documents describes how to perform the decomposition; the directory tree in `01-routing.md` contains a lone `annotations.rs` entry, the only mention in the entire batch                                     | The P5 steps are supplied in this document (see below)                                                                                                          |
| G2  | **The three `include!` transformation sites have conflicting ownership and no implementation**. RFC lists them under P4; `02` assigns its own reference to itself; `06` S7 also lists them. However, the change list in `02` (6 additions + 20 modifications) **does not include `semantic_tokens.rs`**                                                               | The implementation steps are assigned to §P5 5.1 in this document (after P4 is complete); the references in RFC and 06 have been synchronized to point here     |
| G3  | **Three coexisting lists of CI scripts**. `RFC` lists four `scripts/ci/*.py`; `01` lists four scripts + 1 `cargo test`; `07` separately lists four checks (all with different names); `04` also requires `check-synth-boundary.py`. After deduplication there are **at least 9 new scripts, with no unified list**; `check-obligations.py` is owned by both P4 and P9 | See the unified list in §P9 of this document (11 scripts, each owned by one phase); all other documents refer to that table                                     |
| G4  | **Conflict over the fate of `precedence_inline.rs`**. `06` counts it among the “1005 lines that must be revived,” while B6 in the same document says it “must be deleted together with the `Precedence` enum”; `05` argues for deletion                                                                                                                               | **Revive in P1, delete with Pratt in P8** (decision registered as C1)—first restore CI coverage, then perform the deletion in 8.6 alongside the dead ladder     |
| G5  | **Conflicting statements of the L2 goal**. RFC and this directory's index say “grammar-driven syntax,” whereas `05` self-assesses: “**It did not actually adopt true grammar-driven parsing (LALRPOP); it only achieved declarative table lookup**”                                                                                                                   | Consolidated as **complete LALRPOP grammar-driven parsing** (`05` §Syntax: complete grammar-driven parsing); the G7/G8 acceptance gates enforce that commitment |

## Target Design: Phase Sequence

```
P0  仓库维护机制与代码放置规程     08        ← 新增，先立规矩
P1  复活孤儿测试并修缺陷           06 §S1
P2  建立等价性判据基线             07 全部
P3  修正确性漏洞（最小方案）        02 §S3-漏洞半部
P4  阶段契约与统一 Driver          02
P5  checker 文件内拆分             02 + 本文补齐
P6  类型表示单一化                 03
P7  中间表示 SSA 化                04      } 可并行
P8  前端范式变更                   05      }
P9  防反弹门禁（含统一脚本清单）     01 §防反弹 + 08 §可机检规则
P10 其余清理与状态修正             06 §S2/S3/S4/S5/S6
```

**Rationale for adding P0**: the three prohibitions (no fabrication / no endless filling / do not
patch what should be refactored) constrain every action in P1–P10. The rules must be established
before implementation begins; otherwise, while reviving tests in P1, work would already start adding
`mod` declarations to `tests/mod.rs`—precisely the cause of the G4 conflict.

The change = reduce `stderr` to `diagnostics: [[code, file, line], …]` + retain only **crash marker lines** (panic / ICE / backtrace header), and add `strip_ansi` (color codes on the run side) plus lookahead for positions (the position is still found when source-code fragments appear between the diagnostic header and `-->`); `exit` / `stdout` / `check.*` / `expectation` remain unchanged                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | 0.5.1        | The 356-line baseline is regenerated and the `check-corpus-parity` diff is empty; **rerunning the probe after changing locale text still produces zero diff** (rationale demonstrated through actual measurement); mutation check: changing one `run.exit` to 99 reports one diff; cost = the corpus layer no longer verifies runtime text, which is instead covered by locale unit tests / `yx explain` snapshots / generated-artifact reconciliation; **Owning phase: P0; Blocks the mainline: No**                                                                              |
|                                                            | 0.5.2 Channel wiring and calibration: the pre-commit `cargo test` hook and CI switch to the `fast` (before local commits) and `full` (before pushes and in CI) dual channels, and the 2600-tier resource profile is calibrated through actual measurements (currently extrapolated), with a detailed section on resource markers and exclusive tiers added to `test-specification.md` (**pending scheduling**; follow-up slice of #486)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | 0.5.1        | The pre-commit `cargo test` hook no longer runs the full suite without qualification (it currently runs the full suite via `cargo test --all`; the “common commands” section of AGENTS.md specifies the fast/full dual channels and the red line against running the full `--ignored` suite unqualified); CI and pre-commit local checks use the same channel semantics; the peak and duration markers for the 2600 tier are measured rather than extrapolated; `test-specification.md` contains the corresponding detailed section; **Owning phase: P0; Blocks the mainline: No** |

> **There is no task for a size baseline**. The decision of 2026-10-03 removed all line-count /
> volume gates; size problems are addressed through separation of responsibilities (see prohibition
> two, criterion A, in `08`; manual judgment). **0.2.1 is the core acceptance criterion for this
> phase**: if the gate cannot report the known parallel representations, the criterion design is
> wrong and the gate is false.

### P3 Fix Correctness Defects (Minimal Approach)

Source: [02](02-stage-contract.md) §Implementation Points S3, defect-fix half + **2026-10-07
silent-drop-point audit (3.4)**

> **Semantic-correction routing (D59, user decision of 2026-10-11)**: if this track or a later phase
> discovers a defect for which “the old behavior does not count as success”—**A silent failure / B
> silent mismatch / C undefined behavior and uncontrolled resources** (crash, stack overflow,
> silently incorrect answer)—it **may be fixed along with the refactoring**, but all four
> requirements must be met: ① red before green (the red criterion must exist first and genuinely
> fail); ② no change to the externally observable behavior of programs that “previously compiled
> successfully and ran correctly” (they must not start failing, and existing diagnostic codes /
> messages / exit codes must not be removed or modified); ③ same-commit registration (this row
> identifies the error type + the phase revision note + a required “error type” line in the PR
> template); ④ regenerate the baseline in the same commit + manually review the diff. **D
> user-visible semantics / E external-contract table / F RFC-level design changes still require
> prior user decision.** The expanded criteria and A–F whitelist are in
> [07 §Semantic-correction routing criteria](07-equivalence-oracle.md); **the “default
> super-refactor + post-hoc registration” approach is not adopted** (the rationale is in RFC-039
> D59: a zero-red criterion cannot distinguish a fix from a regression and conflicts with “C5′ does
> not exist” and “no relaxations whatsoever”).

      wholesale to 3.21 (level-3 tasks renamed 3.21.1–3.21.4, with the issue anchors unchanged);
      **3.21.1 completed on 2026-10-25** (commit `cfb3442d`, criterion chain and remaining work are
      detailed below and in [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)); **3.21.2 completed on 2026-10-25** (commit
      `df89d6ee`, all three category-D changes fixed with user approval; remaining work is in
      [#509](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/509)); **3.21.3 completed on 2026-10-25**
      (commit `a8ad0530`, criterion chain and remaining work are detailed below and in
      [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)); **3.21.4 completed on 2026-10-25** (commit `3b6df553`, the
      fourth hand-written documentation contradiction remains in
      [#509](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/509)); **the entire group was archived on
      2026-10-25**: all four level-3 tasks passed verification/review and were pushed, all local
      gates were green, and each batch's CI conclusion was reconciled according to coverage overlap
      (running jobs were incorporated into the pre-release unified reconciliation; see each issue's
      closeout comment); **Owning phase: P3; Blocks the mainline: No**)

>

- [ ] **3.22 Decomposition semantics for function-type annotations** (1 level-3 task, registered on
      2026-10-24, issue [#501](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/501); source = follow-up
      from batch-review finding F4 in round 2 of 4.10; **3.22.1 pending scheduling**—unify the
      decomposition of annotations that “return a function type,” so that there is a valid way to
      write “a function whose return type is a function type”; **Owning phase: P3; Blocks the
      mainline: No**)

- [ ] **3.23 Silent degradation on manifest parse failure / missing required fields** (1 level-3
      task, registered on 2026-10-25, issue
      [#503](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/503); source = measurement during 4.9.8
      implementation—when `yaoxiang.toml` fails to parse (invalid TOML) or lacks a required field
      such as `[package] version`, the CLI **does not report a manifest error** and silently
      degrades to single-file semantics ⇒ the cross-file reference pool becomes ineffective ⇒ false
      W1002 reports ×2 (`a.yx:1:1` / `b.yx:1:1`, with both types referenced by the entry point),
      while `check` still exits 0; pre-fix and post-4.9.8-fix behavior is identical item by item
      (predates #465 and is unrelated to that fix); D59 **category D** (externally observable
      behavior), semantics pending decision (A: report an error and exit / B: continue but emit a W
      code / C: preserve the status quo); **Owning phase: P3; Blocks the mainline: No**)

> **Batch (user decision of 2026-10-14)**: the four small items 3.13 / 3.14 / 3.15 / 3.16 are
> **highly related** (method binding, overload decisions, and the inference entry point belong to
> the same path; 3.16 is another carrier of the same “named argument” criterion—the stdlib native
> export), **and are merged into one team**—the team scope is broadened from “one level-2 task” to
> “a batch of related small items”; the batch composition, rationale, and deviations must be
> registered at the team's closeout (see the record in [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)).

**Acceptance**: vulnerability criteria turn green, and **deliberately removing the fix must turn
them red again**. **Risk**: adding E4018 to the multi-file path is a breaking change.

**3.4 acceptance**: 3.4.1 / 3.4.2 must have red criteria first (changing code first is forbidden);
3.4.3 requires manual review; 3.4.4–3.4.8 are governed by C2/C3 and the P9 9.2 gate. **Risk**: the
new diagnostic in 3.4.1 is a behavior change, so the corpus differential baseline and CHANGELOG must
be updated.

> **Emergency path (decision of 2026-10-05)**: P3 does not have to wait for all of P2. The only hard
> prerequisites for 3.1/3.2 are **2.3.2 (multi-file corpus layer) + 2.4.1/2.4.3 (two red
> criteria)**; 2.1/2.2/2.3.1/2.3.3 (IR verifier, snapshots, single-file differential, performance
> baseline) can proceed in parallel with P3. Stopping the bleeding for a correctness defect
> (`Sorted(3)` passing silently) must not be blocked by criteria-infrastructure construction. 3.3
> (panic-to-diagnostic conversion) has no prerequisite and can land at any time.

### External Track RFC-029g: Remove `pub` and Automatic Binding (External Track / #399 — Ordering Constraint D56)

Source: [RFC-029g](../../rfc/accepted/029g-remove-pub-and-auto-bind.md) (accepted on 2026-10-02;
implementation not started), [#399](https://github.com/ChenXu233/YaoXiang/issues/399). **This track
is not a design item of the refactoring itself; it is a specification-conformance deletion forced by
an ordering constraint**; it is distinct from P10 S5 “reduce the visibility of `pub` items”
(narrowing Rust's `pub(crate)`).

**Why it must be completed wholesale before P4**: 029g's deletion surface overlaps the rewriting
surface of four refactoring phases. Reversing the order means repeating the same deletions and
baseline updates on the new grammar / new checker layout / new SSA IR.

| AST `is_pub` ×2 (`Assign` / `TypeDefinition`)                       | `parser/ast.rs:247/270`                                                                                            | **P6 6.5 AST dead variants** + P8 + D1 directory rename (`parser/ast.rs` → top-level `src/ast/`)                                                                                                                                                          |
| `auto_bind_to_type` and the pub branch of `collect_exports`         | `typecheck/checker.rs:2150/3802/3836`, `environment.rs:284`                                                        | **P5** (`include!`→true `mod` and checker decomposition are **pure moves**, and the pub branch will be moved unchanged into the new file)                                                                                                                 |
| Dead-code exemptions `exempt_pub` / `is_exported` and role dispatch | `typecheck/passes/dead_code.rs`, `module/orchestrator.rs:389-407` (inside `check_project`)                         | **P4 4.2.2** (`check_project` slimming)                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Public` modifier in `semantic_tokens`                              | `typecheck/checker/semantic_tokens.rs:450/498`                                                                     | **P4 4.2.6** + P5 + 4.6 (D55)                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

**Acceptance (behavior changes, red criterion first)**:

1. **Red before green**: each of the two behavior changes must land a red criterion before code is
   changed—① `pub` changes from “silently accepted” to a parse error; ② an unreferenced top-level
   binding in a Lib/Script role newly reports W1001 (pub items were previously always exempt).
2. **Test count must not decrease**: when deleting the `exempt_pub` cases in
   `typecheck/passes/tests/dead_code.rs` and the `is_pub` fixture, they must be replaced by
   equivalent new cases.
3. **Baseline in the same commit**: diagnostic-set changes (new parse error + new W1001) ⇒ update
   the corpus differential baseline (`tests/baselines/corpus-parity.jsonl`) and CHANGELOG in the
   same commit; zero IR snapshot diff is expected, and any non-zero diff requires an explanation.
4. **Independent revert unit**: the entire batch occupies one commit group and must not be mixed
   with another phase (DoD 6).
5. `cargo test` / `clippy -D warnings` / `python scripts/rfc/check_tracking.py` all green.

**Ordering**: it overlaps 3.4 in files (`checker.rs`, `environment.rs`) ⇒ the two must run serially
and must not run in parallel; **the whole track must precede P4** (RFC-039 D56). The 029g track does
not depend on 3.4; the implementer decides which goes first based on available context.

> **Commit cross-reference (2026-10-08)**: the incorporation of this section (the P3.5 chapter +
> main-chain/constraint row) and RFC-039's D56 were inadvertently included in `97071c81b` while
> parallel workstreams were cleaning the workspace. That commit's message records “3.4 addendum
> registration + D57/D58 decision filing” and does not mention this track. History is not rewritten;
> it is registered in place according to the established convention (the `d5b18390` precedent, see
> the P2 implementation note) as the basis for later bisecting.

Current mechanism (code walkthrough + measurement on 2026-10-16): `compute_reachability` puts only the full name into `reachable`, while `is_reachable` searches the same set by **short name** for methods ⇒ methods never enter the reachable set (so every method is reported when there is no pool path); `all_defs` uses the full name as its key ⇒ same-named overloads overwrite each other, and only the last can be reported. **Hard constraint**: `collect_used_in_type` is used across modules by `inference/statements.rs:16`.
**If** it is migrated with the annotation family in the future, **then** `checker.rs` must add
`pub(crate) use annotations::*;` to re-export it, leaving that import unchanged; measurement shows
that 5.3 did **not** migrate it, so this batch added no re-export (the criterion was already
satisfied).

**Only split confirmed large blocks.** Do not split modules that are only 200–400 lines—this
increases navigation cost, reduces cohesion, and provides zero decoupling benefit. The remaining
submodules (signatures / type_defs / imports) wait until their files grow back to 1,500 lines.

**Hard acceptance** (**revised on 2026-10-27, inheriting user decision ② of 2026-10-26**):
`git diff --stat tests/yaoxiang tests/baselines src/std/` must be **empty in every round from 6.1
through 6.7**. **Direct-driver test files** under `tests/` (tests that drive the code being changed,
such as `tests/formatter_integration.rs`) **may** be modified when mechanically required by the
change, but each modification must be registered with **file / line count / reason**; **tests must
not be deleted and assertions must not be weakened**. **Exception**: 6.8.2 is a **behavior-fix**
batch (new diagnostic codes + baseline update), is not subject to this hard acceptance criterion,
and is handled as a separate round with separate approval.

> **Revision relationship**: the original wording (user decision ② of 2026-10-26) said
> `git diff --stat tests/ src/std/`, treating all of `tests/` as requiring zero differences; the
> revised wording **only changes direct-driver test files from “zero differences” to “may be changed
> but must be registered with file / line count / reason”**, while the **zero-difference
> requirements for the corpus layer (`tests/yaoxiang`), baseline layer (`tests/baselines`), and
> standard library (`src/std/`) remain unchanged**. First application = 6.1.2: only
> `tests/formatter_integration.rs` changed, by **3 lines** (2 `assert_format_eq` changes from
> `i64`→`Int64` + 1 comment line), because the expected type name in the assertion must track the
> changed spelling; no tests were deleted and no assertions were weakened. See 09a “P6 6.1
> Name-normalization closeout.”

| 8.8 Complete tests                           | 8.8.1 Add associativity cases (**red before green**)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | 8.2.x                            | C5                                                                                                                                            |
|                                              | 8.8.2 Add the four categories of literal-error paths                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | —                                | Cover previously untested paths                                                                                                               |

| 8.9 Normalize parameter-position forms (D53) | 8.9.1 Grammar production `Param ::= Identifier ':' TypeExpr \| TypeExpr`: a bare identifier is resolved in the type namespace, and an unresolved type reports E; an unnamed signature requires the lambda head to carry parameter names (RFC-007:47 existing rule); reconcile RFC-010's form table and interface example (`(Surface)`) with the batch | 8.3.2 + 6.8.1                    | Corpus survey finds zero migrations (all bare identifiers in std/the corpus are real types); an unknown type identifier receives a new E code |

**Risk**: the 8.4 dual-parser differential is an **unskippable equivalence gate**. If it produces a
non-equivalence, the problem must be resolved before the 8.5 switch rather than masked after the
switch through C5′. If the approximately 89 BP-reference migrations in 8.6 miss a site,
associativity will **silently change** (no error, only a different parse result).

> **8.3–8.6 depend on D0/D2 in `08`**: if the grammar file is placed outside `parser/`, or the
> action code refers back to `sema`, it will be blocked by `check-boundary.py`.

### P9 Anti-Rebound Gates (Unified Script List — Original Gap G3 Supplemented by This Document)

Source: [01](01-routing.md) §Anti-Rebound Mechanism + [08](08-maintenance-mechanism.md)
§Machine-Checkable Rules

**This is the sole authoritative list of all CI scripts** (11 scripts). Scripts are connected to CI
at their “introduction phase”; report-only scripts are made strict uniformly in P9 (changing from
“report” to “fail”)—existing violations (parallel representations, disambiguation aliases, etc.) are
not cleared until P6/P8, and making the gate strict early would leave CI permanently red and cause
the gate to be bypassed manually; when P9 makes the gate strict, it should turn exactly green. P2's
2.5, P4's 4.3.2, and P7's 7c.1 all refer to this table and do not establish separate lists.

- [ ] **9.1–9.11 Connect the eleven scripts at their introduction phases, then make them strict
      uniformly in P9** (details are in the table below)
- [x] **9.12 Prerequisite for making the gate strict: rebaseline criterion-C wiring points and clear
      stale inventory** (2 level-3 tasks, **registered and closed out on 2026-10-26**): the title
      used at registration, “clear existing same-name multiple definitions,” was a **mislabel**—the
      `--strict` violations come only from criterion C (entry-point wiring), while criterion A
      (same-name definitions, 181 entries) is report-only and never makes `--strict` fail; this was
      corrected during closeout. Root cause: `STAGE_MARKERS` was calibrated on 2026-10-06 against
      the pre-P4 state where “six paths were each wired manually”; its comment explicitly states
      that “after P4 unifies the Driver, this table is deleted along with the inventory,” but
      neither the vocabulary nor the 19 `KNOWN_WIRING_SITES` was retired after P4 landed. The
      criterion therefore judged the Driver's **own phase table** (`Stage::DeadCodeAnalysis` variant
      name, `slug` string table, `state.rs` dependency table) to be violations, exactly opposite to
      prohibition three, criterion C's rule text (“adding an Nth entry point **instead of
      registering it in the declarative phase table** → violation”). Disposition = **rebaseline the
      criterion** rather than add a whitelist. Result: `--strict` violations 9 → 0, inventory 19 →
      **7**, measured wiring points 18 → **8** (7 inventory + 1 dispatch point), criterion-A
      same-name definitions 181 → 180 (the `build_registry` entry was deleted), `pub(crate)` 167
      → 166. **Owning phase: P9; Blocks the mainline: No (blocks making P9 strict).**
  - 9.12.1 Rebaseline delivery (four commits, each detailed in the closeout record in
    [09a](./09a-wbs-history.md)): ① **rebaseline the criterion**—merge LEX into `Parsing`
    (`src/driver/stage.rs` and 02 §Target Design 1 both state that `Parsing` = “lexical + syntactic
    analysis (lex → parse)”), recognize DEADCODE/MONO only by call shape, exempt `#[cfg(test)] mod`
    by **brace pairing**, list `dispatch` separately as `DECLARED_DISPATCH_POINTS`, and clear 13
    stale inventory entries (each with a removal rationale, divided by measurement into three
    categories: 5 deleted functions / 5 functions that still exist but have no phase primitive / 3
    entries reduced to a single phase only because LEX was merged); ② **derive the arm
    shape**—derive the 12 variants' `self.<arm>(` calls from `dispatch`'s exhaustive match
    (`Stage::ALL` takes the variants, a single source) + add three fail-loud guards (invalid variant
    source / arm not found / arm has no `fn` definition), and fix the false positive caused by
    counting the signature line as part of the measured body so that `fn check_project(` matched TC
    itself (`fn_body` cuts off the signature section; the measured reduction of “≥2 → <2 phases” is
    0); ③ **change coverage declarations into facts**—the phase implementations of 4 variants
    (VendorConsistency / Discovery / Registry / RoleClassification) are shared primitives outside
    the Driver and are truthfully registered as known blind spots; ④ **describe blind spots by
    nature**—live wiring (`collect_package_refs`, called at `driver/arms.rs:626`) and dead-code
    candidates (`build_registry`, zero callers) must not receive the same disposition. Three
    independent reviews all judged needs_revision (medium → medium → low, with all 3 findings
    genuinely fixed), and the fourth round **passed** with no findings. **Acceptance**:
    `python scripts/ci/check-fanout.py --strict` exit 0 (violations 0 / inventory 6 / dispatch
    point 1) ✅
  - 9.12.2 Expand criterion-C coverage and clear `build_registry` (**completed and closed out on
    2026-10-26**; user decisions **include D1-A and delete D2-A**): ① **delete** the zero-caller
    chain `build_registry` / `build_registry_from` (formerly `orchestrator.rs:379-396`) and
    `discover` (formerly `:416-418`), which consequently lost its only caller; `DiscoveredFile` /
    `Discovery` / `extract_module_info` / `parse_file` are all retained; before deletion,
    `build_registry` was separately approved as an external API-surface change because
    `src/lib.rs:27 pub mod frontend` → `frontend/module/mod.rs:20 pub mod orchestrator` is a public
    lib-target item. ② **retain** the phase primitives outside the Driver that still exist after
    deletion: VendorConsistency ← `ensure_vendor_consistency`, Discovery ← `discover_with_used`,
    RoleClassification ← `roles::classify` (narrowed to a qualified shape—a bare name would hit
    unrelated same-named helpers, as empirically demonstrated), Registry ← `extract_module_info` (do
    not use bare `.register(`, which has several same-named locations in the repository). ③ Exactly
    one newly exposed location: `collect_package_refs` (`driver/helpers.rs:1057`, live code, called
    at `driver/arms.rs:626`) is registered as inventory **6 → 7**, and measured wiring points 7 → 8.
    ④ The criterion docstring's coverage declaration was changed to facts: the old “6+6” split was
    merged into **10+2**, the R1–R3 determinable inclusion criteria were added, “every function that
    constructs this phase's data is included” was deleted because 5 table entries disprove it, and
    three stale line numbers / unmeasured numbers and two inaccurate comments were fixed
    (`orchestrator.rs:7` module header, `statements.rs:737` reference to the deleted function).
    Behavior-neutral: all 356 corpus rows are byte-for-byte identical, 216 snapshots have zero
    drift, `cargo test --lib` 2855/0/2, `pub(crate)` 167 → 166 (the inevitable result of deleting
    `discover`). Review chain: round 1 needs_revision (1 medium + 2 low) → fix → round 2 **pass**
    (independently reproduced with a self-built probe); subsequent F4/F5/F6/F8 cleanup round →
    narrow review **pass** (AST statement-by-statement comparison confirms no collateral changes to
    the criterion). **Owning phase: P9; Blocks the mainline: No.**

## Implementation Points: Dependencies and Parallelization

### Mandatory Serial Main Chain

`P0 → P1 → P2 → P3 → P3.5 → P4 → P5 → P6 → (P7 ∥ P8) → P9 → P10`

> **P3 emergency path (decision of 2026-10-05)**: P3's prerequisites are narrowed to **2.3.2 +
> 2.4.1 + 2.4.3** (the multi-file corpus layer + two red criteria). The rest of P2 (2.1 IR verifier,
> 2.2 snapshots, 2.3.1/2.3.3 differential and performance baseline) can proceed in parallel with P3.
> Stopping the bleeding for a correctness defect must not be blocked by criteria-infrastructure
> construction; however, **P4 must still wait for all of P2**—the unified Driver requires all three
> layers of criteria to be in place.

| Constraint                | Rationale                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| P0 → P1                   | The three prohibitions constrain every action in P1 (1.2.x is precisely the act of adding a `mod` declaration to `mod.rs`)                                                                                                                                         |
| P1 → P2                   | Reviving tests changes the test count and corpus baseline                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| P2 (after narrowing) → P3 | Vulnerability criteria must be **written in red first** (2.4.1/2.4.3) + the multi-file corpus layer (2.3.2) must exist so that red tests have somewhere to run                                                                                                     |
| P3 → P4                   | Fix bugs before refactoring; reversing the order causes bugs to be固化 by the phase table as “established behavior”                                                                                                                                                |
| P3.5 → P4                 | RFC-029g (#399)'s deletion surface covers the respective rewriting targets of P4 4.2.2 / P5 / P6 / P7 / P8 (the list is in §P3.5 of this document)—reversing the order means repeating the same deletions on the new grammar, new checker layout, and new IR (D56) |
| P4 → P5                   | The `include!` transformation and checker decomposition modify the same file                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| P5 → P6                   | Consecutive changes to the same file must be separated; otherwise, regressions cannot be located by bisection                                                                                                                                                      |
| P6 → P7 / P8              | If type representations do not converge first, the new IR will grow into a third representation                                                                                                                                                                    |
| P7/P8 → P9                | The snapshot baseline in 9.5 and the `pub(crate)` leak count in 9.3 reach their final form only after P7/P8                                                                                                                                                        |

### Parallelization Groups

| Group                                  | Members                                                                            | Rationale                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| -------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **A (the only one authorized by RFC)** | P7 ∥ P8                                                                            | Disjoint file sets: `ir.rs`/`ir_gen.rs`/`bytecode.rs`/`translator.rs` vs `lexer/*`/`parser/*`                                                                                                                                                         |
| B                                      | 1.2.1 ∥ 1.2.2 ∥ 1.2.3 ∥ 1.2.4                                                      | Each independently adds a `mod`; revert file by file                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| C                                      | 2.1 ∥ 2.2 ∥ 2.3.1 ∥ 2.3.2 ∥ 2.4                                                    | The three layers of criteria are independent                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| D                                      | 4.4 (proof-layer wrap-up) ∥ the entire P6 track                                    | `02` explicitly states in S5 that it “should proceed independently of S1-S4”                                                                                                                                                                          |
| E                                      | 4.2.5 ∥ 4.2.6 ∥ 4.2.8                                                              | All are mutually independent after 4.1 is complete                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| F                                      | 9.3 ∥ 9.9 ∥ 9.10 (the three scripts introduced in P0 can be developed in parallel) | The snapshot baseline in 9.5 must be taken last                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| G                                      | S3 ∥ S4 ∥ S5                                                                       | Disjoint file sets (verified on 2026-10-05: S3 modifies `docs/src/design/check/` + `config.js`; S4 modifies `docs/src/rfc/` + the `check_tracking.py` generator; S5 modifies two `pub` locations in `src/frontend/core/typecheck/`; no pair overlaps) |

**Cannot run in parallel (file overlap)**: P1(1.1.x) ∥ P8(8.0.x); 1.2.1 ∥ 8.3.2; 6.0 ∥ S2 (the same
files in the B6/E5 batch); 4.3.2 ∥ 9.2 (already unified under P4); **3.4 ∥ P3.5** (`checker.rs` /
`environment.rs` are the same files—the external track's pub-deletion batch and the silent-channel
fixes must run serially).

**New internal documentation constraint**: 6.0→6.1→6.2→6.3→6.4→6.5→6.6 is strictly serial;
7a→7b→7c→7d is strictly serial, and 7b's `Phi` arm must precede 7d.

## Key Decisions and Rationale

- **P0 is a separate phase rather than part of P9**—gates are “post-hoc checks,” whereas rules are
  “pre-hoc judgment.” P9's scripts cannot prevent “patching what should have been refactored”; only
  the D0–D4 review checklist can.
- **P9 is the sole authoritative list of CI scripts**—the original gap G3's three coexisting lists
  made closure impossible. This table merges them into 11 scripts, each owned by one phase, with
  report-only status and strict-transition timing explicitly traceable.
- **P3.5 incorporates RFC-029g rather than leaving it as an “independent track to do after the
  refactoring”**—its deletion surface overlaps the rewriting surfaces of P4/P5/P6/P7/P8 (RFC-039
  D56). Deferring it until after the refactoring means repeating the same deletions on the new
  grammar, new checker layout, and new IR; this is the same lesson as incorporating `2315`/`2341`
  into 7e and RFC-status correction into S4.
- **P5's steps are supplied by this document rather than merged into P4**—P4 is already the largest
  single-point risk, and adding checker decomposition would exceed the granularity that can be
  rolled back.
- **5.3.2 uses “zero caller changes” as its hard acceptance criterion**—the decomposition must be
  independently revertible.

## Known Limitations and Risks

- **1.3.x (fixing the exposed defects) has no regression criterion**—it only says “the fixed defect
  must not be rolled back” and lacks a recording requirement identifying “what was fixed.”
  **Execution convention**: the commit message for every 1.3.x fix must cite the name of the fixed
  test and the defect description, as a basis for later bisection.
- ~~**7b.1's granularity is too coarse**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: split into two exclusive commits,
  7b.1a / 7b.1b, under DoD 6.
- **8.2.1's granularity is too coarse**—approximately 89 renamed references; missing one **silently
  changes associativity**.
- ~~**D17 / D20 have no corresponding level-3 tasks**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: D17 is implemented
  by 7e.1, and D20 by 4.5.1/4.5.2; D32/D34 opcode reclamation is incorporated into 7e.1, while the
  deletion remains under P10 S6.
- ~~**The `2315` / `2341` data-loss defects were originally planned as an “independent issue”**~~
  **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: incorporated as 7e.2 / 7e.3. The exception table (throw/try) is core
  language semantics and must not be left as a future implementation.
- ~~**E2 (`lib.rs:568/582` `todo!()`) has no phase ownership**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**:
  incorporated into P10 S2.
- ~~**F7 (opcode gate extractor) has ambiguous ownership**~~ **Fixed (2026-10-05)**: incorporated
  into P10 S6's generation-time gate task.

## Conflict Registration: All Resolved

> **The original “conflict registration” C1–C8 and the open questions in the various documents have
> all converged into the
> [RFC-039 decision registry](../../rfc/accepted/039-compiler-architecture.md) (D1–D50).** This
> document retains no pending items. The mapping is:

| Original number | Issue                                                                          | Decision                                                                                                                                                           |
| --------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| C1              | `precedence_inline.rs` is revived in P1 and deleted in P8                      | **Revive** (the premise of P1 is to revive tests with real assertions), and P8's 8.6 deletes the file together with Pratt                                          |
| C2              | “Grammar-driven” commitment vs table-driven implementation                     | **Fully implemented**: use LALRPOP; see “Syntax: complete grammar-driven parsing” in `05`                                                                          |
| C3              | Conflict between the C6 category definition and S1 classification              | **S1 is classified under C6** (revival requires only regression tests, not equivalence criteria); the C6 definition is expanded to “pure deletion or pure revival” |
| C4              | `test_release_plan_spans_consumed` whitelist                                   | **No whitelist**; the difference must be empty; if it cannot be empty, the `ReleasePlan` contract is defective → change it to `PlanId` (D20/D41)                   |
| C5              | Internal differences between the two `Aggregation` functions were not verified | **P4's 4.2.7 lists this as a mandatory verification item**, to be confirmed line by line during implementation                                                     |
| C6              | The wasm playground fix requires P4's `ProgramKind`                            | **The wasm-path fix is incorporated into 4.2.8**; P3 handles only the three native entry points                                                                    |
| C7              | Ownership of `method_def_ordinals`                                             | **Assigned to 04** (P7 batch c), see D21                                                                                                                           |
| C8              | Correction of `upvalue_count` and the `.42` version number                     | **Fix + change `VERSION` from 4 to 5** (the format header already has `MAGIC` + `VERSION` fields, `codegen/bytecode.rs:14-16`), see D17                            |
